container.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eRequirements\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca Pinned for reproducibility.\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eTrial image\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e\u003ccode\u003eghcr.io/scaleapi/drugdiscoverybench:1.0.2-lightweight\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003ePulled in \u003ca href=\"#1-pull-the-docker-image-6-gb-1030-min\"\u003estep 1\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003c/tbody\u003e\n\u003c/table\u003e\u003c/markdown-accessiblity-table\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eHost resources:\u003c/strong\u003e the image is \u003cstrong\u003e~6 GB compressed to pull but ~23 GB on disk\u003c/strong\u003e (\u003cstrong\u003e30+ GB free recommended\u003c/strong\u003e); each task can use up to \u003cstrong\u003e8 GB RAM\u003c/strong\u003e (\u003cstrong\u003e16 GB total recommended\u003c/strong\u003e).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eTime:\u003c/strong\u003e first-time setup ~30–60 min (the ~6 GB pull dominates); overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"# --- Agent credentials (default agent is claude-code) ---\nexport ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=\u0026quot;your-key\u0026quot;\n\n# Web search: See\n\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ca href=\"/scaleapi/DrugDiscoveryBench/blob/main/docs/model-proxy.md\"\u003edocs/model-proxy.md\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e for a portable LiteLLM \u003ccode\u003econfig.yaml\u003c/code\u003e,\nper-CLI specifics, the Codex Responses-API requirement, and the caveats.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e\u003cb\u003eUsing Harbor CLI directly\u003c/b\u003e\u003c/summary\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe wrapper script is a convenience layer over \u003ccode\u003eharbor run\u003c/code\u003e. Call Harbor directly for full control:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"harbor run \\\n    -p benchmark/tasks/69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812 \\\n    -m claude-sonnet-4-6 -a claude-code \\\n    -e docker \\\n    --ae \u0026quot;ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY\u0026quot; The agent CLI runs inside the image\nand invokes the bundled biomedical tools directly from Python/Bash (every tool is\nimportable as \u003ccode\u003ebiomni.tool.\u0026lt;domain\u0026gt;.\u0026lt;name\u0026gt;\u003c/code\u003e); \u003ccode\u003e/workspace\u003c/code\u003e is the shared working\ndirectory and the data lake is mounted read-only when available. When the agent is\ndone it writes its final answer to \u003ccode\u003e/workspace/answer.md\u003c/code\u003e, and the judge grades that\nanswer against the task's \u003ccode\u003erubrics.json\u003c/code\u003e. No network hop to a remote tool API — the\nagent and the full tool surface live in the same container.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" can read from a local \"data lake\" — copies of major biomedical\ndatabases baked into the image — for fast, deterministic, \u003cstrong\u003eoffline\u003c/strong\u003e lookups.\nThe image comes in three tiers that trade size for offline coverage:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cmarkdown-accessiblity-table\u003e\u003ctable\u003e\n\u003cthead\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003cth\u003eTier (default)\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eThe Biomni \u003cstrong\u003edata-lake files used by the eval\u003c/strong\u003e (read-only at \u003ccode\u003e/workspace/data/biomni_data/data_lake/\u003c/code\u003e) — curated flat-file datasets (BindingDB, GTEx, DepMap, GWAS Catalog, MSigDB, …). Heavy \u003ccode\u003equery_*\u003c/code\u003e DB clients (PubChem, ChEMBL, UniProt, …) still hit \u003cstrong\u003elive APIs\u003c/strong\u003e.\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e~6 GB / ~23 GB\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003edata-lake files (\u003ccode\u003equery_*\u003c/code\u003e live)\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ccode\u003e1.0.2-core\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e~21 local databases — cBioPortal, UniProt, ChEMBL, ENCODE, Ensembl, ClinicalTrials (AACT), STRING, openFDA, Reactome, GEO, GWAS, PDB, … (\u003ccode\u003eBIOMNI_LOCAL_DB_ONLY=1\u003c/code\u003e)\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e37 GB / 211 GB\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e~74%\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ccode\u003e1.0.2-full\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e\u003ccode\u003ecore\u003c/code\u003e + PubChem, PubMed, AlphaFold, Open Targets\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e136 GB / 570 GB\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e~99.7%\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003c/tbody\u003e\n\u003c/table\u003e\u003c/markdown-accessiblity-table\u003e\n\u003cblockquote\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eAll 82 tasks pin \u003ccode\u003e1.0.2-lightweight\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/strong\u003e It bakes the data-lake files the eval\nuses, so those file reads are offline and reproducible (matching how the benchmark\nwas run). The heavy \u003ccode\u003equery_*\u003c/code\u003e database clients (PubChem, ChEMBL, UniProt, …) still\nhit live public APIs on \u003ccode\u003elightweight\u003c/code\u003e; the \u003ccode\u003ecore\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003efull\u003c/code\u003e tiers bake those\ndatabases in too, serving roughly \u003cstrong\u003e~74%\u003c/strong\u003e and \u003cstrong\u003e~99.7%\u003c/strong\u003e of \u003ccode\u003equery_*\u003c/code\u003e calls\noffline.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/blockquote\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSwitching tiers.\u003c/strong\u003e Tasks ship pinned to \u003ccode\u003elightweight\u003c/code\u003e. <p align="center" dir="auto">
  </p><div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h1 align="center" tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Drug Discovery Agent Benchmark</h1><a id="user-content-drug-discovery-agent-benchmark" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Drug Discovery Agent Benchmark" href="#drug-discovery-agent-benchmark"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto"></p>
<p dir="auto">A benchmark for evaluating AI agents on real drug-discovery and life-sciences
workflows. It contains <strong>82 <a href="https://github.com/harbor-framework/harbor">Harbor</a>-formatted
eval tasks</strong>, each a self-contained biomedical research problem, graded by an LLM
judge against expert-authored rubrics. Tasks run inside a pre-built Docker image
that ships a complete biomedical toolchain, so an agent can pull data, run analyses,
and compute answers entirely within the trial container.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Requirements</h2><a id="user-content-requirements" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Requirements" href="#requirements"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto"><strong>OS:</strong> macOS or Linux (Docker on Windows works but is less tested).</p>
<p dir="auto">Software you install on the <strong>host</strong>:</p>
<markdown-accessiblity-table><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Software</th>
<th>Version</th>
<th>Why / install</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td>Docker (Desktop or Engine)</td>
<td><strong>24+</strong>, with the <strong>Compose v2</strong> plugin</td>
<td>Runs every trial; Harbor's docker executor uses <code>docker compose</code>. Must be <strong>running</strong> before you start. <a href="https://docs.docker.com/get-started/get-docker/" rel="nofollow">Docker Desktop</a> / <a href="https://docs.docker.com/engine/install/" rel="nofollow">Engine</a>. Tested with Docker 29.x.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Python (host)</td>
<td><strong>3.10–3.13</strong></td>
<td>Host interpreter for the Harbor CLI. (LiteLLM, a Harbor dependency, caps at <code>&lt;3.14</code>.) <code>python3 --version</code>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Harbor</td>
<td><strong><code>0.13.1</code></strong></td>
<td>The eval runner — <code>pip install harbor==0.13.1</code> (or <code>uv tool install harbor@0.13.1</code>). Pinned for reproducibility.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Trial image</td>
<td><code>ghcr.io/scaleapi/drugdiscoverybench:1.0.2-lightweight</code></td>
<td>Pulled in <a href="#1-pull-the-docker-image-6-gb-1030-min">step 1</a>.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Host resources:</strong> the image is <strong>~6 GB compressed to pull but ~23 GB on disk</strong> (<strong>30+ GB free recommended</strong>); each task can use up to <strong>8 GB RAM</strong> (<strong>16 GB total recommended</strong>).</p>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Time:</strong> first-time setup ~30–60 min (the ~6 GB pull dominates); a single task run takes up to ~45 min (agent timeout 45 min, judge 10 min).</p>
<p dir="auto">You do <strong>not</strong> install the biomedical toolchain or agent CLIs — they're baked into the <code>1.0.2-lightweight</code> image at pinned versions: conda env (Python 3.11) + R + the BiOMNI tool suite, Node 22, <code>@anthropic-ai/claude-code</code> 2.1.190, <code>@openai/codex</code> 0.122.0, <code>@google/gemini-cli</code> 0.46.0 (see <a href="#whats-in-the-image">What's in the image</a>).</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Quick Start</h2><a id="user-content-quick-start" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Quick Start" href="#quick-start"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">1. Pull the Docker image (~6 GB, 10–30 min)</h3><a id="user-content-1-pull-the-docker-image-6-gb-1030-min" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 1. <div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">2. <div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">3. Get the rubrics</h3><a id="user-content-3-get-the-rubrics" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 3. Get the rubrics" href="#3-get-the-rubrics"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The scoring rubrics and reference answers are <strong>not</strong> stored in this repo — so the
benchmark can be public without leaking answers into training data, they are
released through a gated HuggingFace dataset. Each task's <code>tests/rubrics.json</code>
ships with empty <code>ground_truth</code> / <code>outcome_rubrics</code> / <code>process_rubrics</code>; a helper
script fills them in.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# 1. Request access (one click) and wait for the auto-approval e-mail:
#    https://huggingface.co/datasets/ScaleAI/DrugDiscoveryBench
# 2. Authenticate (cached login, or set HF_TOKEN):
pip install huggingface_hub
huggingface-cli login

# 3. Populate every task's rubrics.json in place:
python scripts/populate_rubrics.py
# (add --dry-run to preview without writing)"><pre><span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> 1. Request access (one click) and wait for the auto-approval e-mail:</span>
<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span>    https://huggingface.co/datasets/ScaleAI/DrugDiscoveryBench</span>
<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> 2. Authenticate (cached login, or set HF_TOKEN):</span>
pip install huggingface_hub
huggingface-cli login

<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> 3. Populate every task's rubrics.json in place:</span>
python scripts/populate_rubrics.py
<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> (add --dry-run to preview without writing)</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Without this step the judge has no rubrics to grade against and every trial scores 0.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">4. Set credentials and run</h3><a id="user-content-4-set-credentials-and-run" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 4. Set credentials and run" href="#4-set-credentials-and-run"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Two LLM roles need credentials:</p>
<markdown-accessiblity-table><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Role</th>
<th>What it does</th>
<th>Passed via</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><strong>Agent</strong></td>
<td>The model being evaluated (runs inside the trial container)</td>
<td>agent env vars (below)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Judge</strong></td>
<td>Grades the agent's answer against rubrics</td>
<td><code>JUDGE_*</code> env vars</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# --- Agent credentials (default agent is claude-code) ---
export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=&quot;your-key&quot;

# Web search: agents' native web tools are disabled by default (they bypass the
# egress denylist), so web search is funneled through Biomni's search_web, which
# needs a Brave key. Without it, agents have no web search.
export BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY=&quot;your-brave-key&quot;   # api.search.brave.com

# --- Judge credentials (any OpenAI-API-compatible endpoint) ---
export JUDGE_BASE_URL=&quot;https://api.openai.com/v1&quot;
export JUDGE_API_KEY=&quot;your-key&quot;
export JUDGE_MODEL=&quot;gpt-4o&quot;     # any model your JUDGE_BASE_URL serves

# Run a single task
scripts/run_eval.sh 69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812

# Run with a specific model and agent
scripts/run_eval.sh 69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --agent claude-code

# Run with Codex
export OPENAI_API_KEY=&quot;your-key&quot;
scripts/run_eval.sh 69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812 --model gpt-5.5 --agent codex

# Run all tasks, 5 trials each, 10 concurrent
scripts/run_eval.sh all -k 5 -n 10"><pre><span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> --- Agent credentials (default agent is claude-code) ---</span>
<span class="pl-k">export</span> ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>your-key<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>

<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> Web search: agents' native web tools are disabled by default (they bypass the</span>
<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> egress denylist), so web search is funneled through Biomni's search_web, which</span>
<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> needs a Brave key. Without it, agents have no web search.</span>
<span class="pl-k">export</span> BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>your-brave-key<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>   <span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> api.search.brave.com</span>

<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> --- Judge credentials (any OpenAI-API-compatible endpoint) ---</span>
<span class="pl-k">export</span> JUDGE_BASE_URL=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>https://api.openai.com/v1<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>
<span class="pl-k">export</span> JUDGE_API_KEY=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>your-key<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>
<span class="pl-k">export</span> JUDGE_MODEL=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>gpt-4o<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>     <span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> any model your JUDGE_BASE_URL serves</span>

<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> Run a single task</span>
scripts/run_eval.sh 69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812

<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> Run with a specific model and agent</span>
scripts/run_eval.sh 69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --agent claude-code

<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> Run with Codex</span>
<span class="pl-k">export</span> OPENAI_API_KEY=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>your-key<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>
scripts/run_eval.sh 69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812 --model gpt-5.5 --agent codex

<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> Run all tasks, 5 trials each, 10 concurrent</span>
scripts/run_eval.sh all -k 5 -n 10</pre></div>
<blockquote>
<p dir="auto"><strong>One proxy, one key:</strong> if you route LLM traffic through a single proxy, set
<code>LLM_API_KEY</code> and <code>LLM_BASE_URL</code> and the per-role <strong>API keys and base URLs</strong> above
are derived automatically (any one you set explicitly still wins). You still set
<code>JUDGE_MODEL</code>, pick the agent model with <code>--model</code>, and — if you want web search —
<code>BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY</code> (a separate Brave service, not the LLM proxy). See
<a href="#running-against-any-model--proxy">Running against any model / proxy</a>.</p>
</blockquote>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">5. Where are my results?</h3><a id="user-content-5-where-are-my-results" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 5. Where are my results?" href="#5-where-are-my-results"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Each run writes to <code>jobs/&lt;timestamp&gt;/&lt;task_id&gt;/</code>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="jobs/&lt;timestamp&gt;/&lt;task_id&gt;/verifier/reward.json          ← your score (0–100)
jobs/&lt;timestamp&gt;/&lt;task_id&gt;/verifier/grades_detail.json   ← per-criterion breakdown
jobs/&lt;timestamp&gt;/&lt;task_id&gt;/agent/                         ← full agent transcript"><pre class="notranslate"><code>jobs/&lt;timestamp&gt;/&lt;task_id&gt;/verifier/reward.json          ← your score (0–100)
jobs/&lt;timestamp&gt;/&lt;task_id&gt;/verifier/grades_detail.json   ← per-criterion breakdown
jobs/&lt;timestamp&gt;/&lt;task_id&gt;/agent/                         ← full agent transcript
</code></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Agent credentials by harness</h2><a id="user-content-agent-credentials-by-harness" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Agent credentials by harness" href="#agent-credentials-by-harness"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">By default each agent calls its own provider's API and only accepts that
provider's model names — <code>claude-code</code> → Anthropic, <code>codex</code> → OpenAI,
<code>gemini-cli</code> → Gemini. To mix and match (e.g. run <code>claude-code</code> on a non-Anthropic
model), front everything with a proxy — see the <a href="#running-against-any-model--proxy">next section</a>.</p>
<markdown-accessiblity-table><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Agent (<code>--agent</code>)</th>
<th>Credential env var</th>
<th>Optional base-URL override</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><code>claude-code</code></td>
<td><code>ANTHROPIC_API_KEY</code></td>
<td><code>ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL</code></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>codex</code></td>
<td><code>OPENAI_API_KEY</code></td>
<td><code>OPENAI_BASE_URL</code> (<code>/v1</code>)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>gemini-cli</code></td>
<td><code>GEMINI_API_KEY</code></td>
<td><code>GOOGLE_GEMINI_BASE_URL</code></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>
<p dir="auto">For <strong>gemini-cli</strong>, the key and base URL must match: with no <code>GOOGLE_GEMINI_BASE_URL</code>,
<code>GEMINI_API_KEY</code> is a Google AI Studio key (<code>AIza…</code>) and calls go direct to Google;
if you set a gateway base URL, <code>GEMINI_API_KEY</code> must be <strong>that gateway's key</strong>.
<code>run_eval.sh</code> defaults the base URL to a LiteLLM proxy <strong>root</strong> (not the <code>/gemini</code>
passthrough) so <code>vertex_ai/…</code> models route to Vertex and <code>google_web_search</code>
grounding uses Vertex quota instead of the Gemini Developer API's 1,500/day cap.
Use a <code>vertex_ai/global/gemini-3.x</code> model for that path. Details: <a href="/scaleapi/DrugDiscoveryBench/blob/main/docs/model-proxy.md#gemini-cli">docs/model-proxy.md</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Running against any model / proxy</h2><a id="user-content-running-against-any-model--proxy" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Running against any model / proxy" href="#running-against-any-model--proxy"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The agent and judge can both point at an LLM <strong>proxy</strong> (e.g. an
<a href="https://docs.litellm.ai/" rel="nofollow">OpenAI-compatible</a> gateway like LiteLLM) instead of
the provider APIs directly. A proxy lets you use <strong>one key for everything</strong>, route
any agent CLI at any backend model, and centralize quota/logging.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# One key + one endpoint for the agent and the judge:
export LLM_API_KEY=&quot;your-proxy-key&quot;
export LLM_BASE_URL=&quot;https://your-proxy.example.com&quot;
# run_eval.sh derives ANTHROPIC_/OPENAI_/GEMINI_/JUDGE_ base URLs + keys from these
# (OpenAI and the judge get a /v1 suffix; Anthropic and Gemini use the root).
# Any per-role var you set explicitly still wins.

# Still set explicitly (not derived from the key):
export JUDGE_MODEL=&quot;gpt-4o&quot;                       # any alias your proxy serves
export BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY=&quot;your-brave-key&quot;      # optional — only if you want web search

# --model is the alias your proxy resolves; the default agent is claude-code.
scripts/run_eval.sh 69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812 --model your-model-alias --agent claude-code"><pre><span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> One key + one endpoint for the agent and the judge:</span>
<span class="pl-k">export</span> LLM_API_KEY=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>your-proxy-key<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>
<span class="pl-k">export</span> LLM_BASE_URL=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>https://your-proxy.example.com<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>
<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> run_eval.sh derives ANTHROPIC_/OPENAI_/GEMINI_/JUDGE_ base URLs + keys from these</span>
<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> (OpenAI and the judge get a /v1 suffix; Anthropic and Gemini use the root).</span>
<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> Any per-role var you set explicitly still wins.</span>

<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> Still set explicitly (not derived from the key):</span>
<span class="pl-k">export</span> JUDGE_MODEL=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>gpt-4o<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>                       <span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> any alias your proxy serves</span>
<span class="pl-k">export</span> BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>your-brave-key<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>      <span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> optional — only if you want web search</span>

<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> --model is the alias your proxy resolves; the default agent is claude-code.</span>
scripts/run_eval.sh 69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812 --model your-model-alias --agent claude-code</pre></div>
<p dir="auto"><strong>What controls the agent</strong> (the model that actually executes the trajectory and
solves the task — distinct from the judge):</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><strong><code>--agent</code></strong> — which CLI <em>harness</em> runs the trajectory: <code>claude-code</code> (default),
<code>codex</code>, or <code>gemini-cli</code>.</li>
<li><strong><code>--model</code></strong> — the model that harness drives, i.e. the alias your proxy resolves.
<strong>This is the problem-solving model.</strong> It's passed straight to the agent CLI inside
the trial container (<code>harbor run -m</code>).</li>
<li><strong><code>LLM_BASE_URL</code> + <code>LLM_API_KEY</code></strong> become <em>that agent's</em> endpoint and key — for
<code>claude-code</code>, <code>ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL</code> → your proxy root and <code>ANTHROPIC_API_KEY</code> → your
key (injected into the container). So the agent's own LLM calls go through your proxy,
and <code>--model</code> can resolve to <strong>any</strong> backend the proxy fronts (including non-Anthropic).</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">This includes the advanced case of driving <strong>Claude Code against a non-Anthropic
model</strong> (Gemini, GPT, …) through a proxy's Anthropic-format endpoint. See
<strong><a href="/scaleapi/DrugDiscoveryBench/blob/main/docs/model-proxy.md">docs/model-proxy.md</a></strong> for a portable LiteLLM <code>config.yaml</code>,
per-CLI specifics, the Codex Responses-API requirement, and the caveats.</p>
<details>
<summary><b>Using Harbor CLI directly</b></summary>
<p dir="auto">The wrapper script is a convenience layer over <code>harbor run</code>. Call Harbor directly for full control:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="harbor run \
    -p benchmark/tasks/69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812 \
    -m claude-sonnet-4-6 -a claude-code \
    -e docker \
    --ae &quot;ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY&quot; \
    --environment-build-timeout-multiplier 3.0 \
    -k 1 -n 1 --yes"><pre>harbor run \
    -p benchmark/tasks/69b025e20c10fe76b7aaf812 \
    -m claude-sonnet-4-6 -a claude-code \
    -e docker \
    --ae <span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<span class="pl-smi">$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY</span><span class="pl-pds">"</span></span> \
    --environment-build-timeout-multiplier 3.0 \
    -k 1 -n 1 --yes</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Key flags:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><code>-p</code> — path to a task directory (repeat for multiple tasks)</li>
<li><code>-m</code> — model name, <code>-a</code> — agent (<code>claude-code</code>, <code>codex</code>, <code>gemini-cli</code>)</li>
<li><code>-e</code> — Harbor executor (<code>docker</code> for local; other backends need <code>HARBOR_EXEC_KWARGS</code>)</li>
<li><code>--ae</code> — pass env vars into the agent container</li>
<li><code>-k</code> — trials per task, <code>-n</code> — max concurrent trials</li>
<li><code>--environment-build-timeout-multiplier 3.0</code> — triples Harbor's image-build/pull
timeout; the ~6 GB image can exceed the default on a slow connection or under
emulation (e.g. Apple Silicon running an amd64 image).</li>
</ul>
<blockquote>
<p dir="auto">If you call Harbor directly (not via <code>run_eval.sh</code>), remember the judge reads
<code>JUDGE_BASE_URL</code> / <code>JUDGE_API_KEY</code> / <code>JUDGE_MODEL</code> from the environment.</p>
</blockquote>
</details>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">How eval tasks work</h2><a id="user-content-how-eval-tasks-work" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: How eval tasks work" href="#how-eval-tasks-work"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Each task is a self-contained Harbor trial. The agent CLI runs inside the image
and invokes the bundled biomedical tools directly from Python/Bash (every tool is
importable as <code>biomni.tool.&lt;domain&gt;.&lt;name&gt;</code>); <code>/workspace</code> is the shared working
directory and the data lake is mounted read-only when available. When the agent is
done it writes its final answer to <code>/workspace/answer.md</code>, and the judge grades that
answer against the task's <code>rubrics.json</code>. No network hop to a remote tool API — the
agent and the full tool surface live in the same container.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">What's in the image</h2><a id="user-content-whats-in-the-image" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: What's in the image" href="#whats-in-the-image"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The <code>drugdiscoverybench:1.0.2-lightweight</code> Docker image (<strong>~6 GB compressed to pull,
~23 GB on disk</strong>) is a self-contained trial environment built on
<a href="https://github.com/snap-stanford/Biomni">BiOMNI</a>, with our own fixes and improvements
on top (dependency/tool repairs, agent-CLI co-location, egress hardening, and more):</p>
<markdown-accessiblity-table><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Component</th>
<th>Details</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td>BiOMNI source</td>
<td>Full Python package with the BiOMNI tool suite across 17 domains</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Conda env</td>
<td><code>biomni_e1</code> with Python 3.11, R, bioinformatics CLI tools</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Agent CLIs</td>
<td>Node 22 — <code>@anthropic-ai/claude-code</code>, <code>@openai/codex</code>, <code>@google/gemini-cli</code> (plus a Grok binary, see <a href="#appendix-grok-cli">Appendix: Grok CLI</a>)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Data lake</td>
<td><code>1.0.2-lightweight</code> (default) bakes the <strong>eval data-lake files</strong> (read-only at <code>/workspace/data/biomni_data/data_lake/</code>); <code>query_*</code> DB clients still hit live APIs. <code>core</code>/<code>full</code> add local <code>query_*</code> databases (see <a href="#data-lake">Data lake</a>)</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Repository Layout</h2><a id="user-content-repository-layout" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Repository Layout" href="#repository-layout"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="benchmark/

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Trial outputs</h2><a id="user-content-trial-outputs" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Trial outputs" href="#trial-outputs"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Each trial writes to <code>jobs/&lt;timestamp&gt;/&lt;task_id&gt;/</code>:</p>
<markdown-accessiblity-table><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>File</th>
<th>Contents</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><code>agent/</code></td>
<td>Full agent transcript, trajectory, session logs</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>verifier/reward.json</code></td>
<td>Numeric scores (Harbor reads this for aggregation)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>verifier/grades_detail.json</code></td>
<td>Per-criterion grades with LLM justifications</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>exception.txt</code></td>
<td>Present only if the trial errored</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Data lake</h2><a id="user-content-data-lake" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Data lake" href="#data-lake"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Biomni tools can read from a local "data lake" — copies of major biomedical
databases baked into the image — for fast, deterministic, <strong>offline</strong> lookups.
The image comes in three tiers that trade size for offline coverage:</p>
<markdown-accessiblity-table><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Tier (tag)</th>
<th>Baked data</th>
<th>Pull / on-disk</th>
<th>Offline DB coverage</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><strong><code>1.0.2-lightweight</code></strong> (default)</td>
<td>The Biomni <strong>data-lake files used by the eval</strong> (read-only at <code>/workspace/data/biomni_data/data_lake/</code>) — curated flat-file datasets (BindingDB, GTEx, DepMap, GWAS Catalog, MSigDB, …). Heavy <code>query_*</code> DB clients (PubChem, ChEMBL, UniProt, …) still hit <strong>live APIs</strong>.</td>
<td>~6 GB / ~23 GB</td>
<td>data-lake files (<code>query_*</code> live)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong><code>1.0.2-core</code></strong></td>
<td>~21 local databases — cBioPortal, UniProt, ChEMBL, ENCODE, Ensembl, ClinicalTrials (AACT), STRING, openFDA, Reactome, GEO, GWAS, PDB, … (<code>BIOMNI_LOCAL_DB_ONLY=1</code>)</td>
<td>37 GB / 211 GB</td>
<td>~74%</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong><code>1.0.2-full</code></strong></td>
<td><code>core</code> + PubChem, PubMed, AlphaFold, Open Targets</td>
<td>136 GB / 570 GB</td>
<td>~99.7%</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>
<blockquote>
<p dir="auto"><strong>All 82 tasks pin <code>1.0.2-lightweight</code>.</strong> It bakes the data-lake files the eval
uses, so those file reads are offline and reproducible (matching how the benchmark
was run). The heavy <code>query_*</code> database clients (PubChem, ChEMBL, UniProt, …) still
hit live public APIs on <code>lightweight</code>; the <code>core</code> and <code>full</code> tiers bake those
databases in too, serving roughly <strong>~74%</strong> and <strong>~99.7%</strong> of <code>query_*</code> calls
offline.</p>
</blockquote>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Switching tiers.</strong> Tasks ship pinned to <code>lightweight</code>. To run the whole suite
against another tier, rewrite every task's <code>docker_image</code> + Dockerfile <code>FROM</code> with:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="python scripts/set_image_tier.py core         # or: full
python scripts/set_image_tier.py lightweight  # switch back (default)"><pre>python scripts/set_image_tier.py core         <span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> or: full</span>
python scripts/set_image_tier.py lightweight  <span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> switch back (default)</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">(Idempotent; <code>--dry-run</code> to preview.)</p>
<details>
<summary><b>Per-database tier membership</b> (which tools answer offline in each tier)</summary>

<blockquote>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Note on the egress healthcheck:</strong> every task's healthcheck expects
<code>http://scale.com</code> to return <code>403</code>, which is served by a proxy baked into the
published <code>ghcr.io/scaleapi/drugdiscoverybench</code> image. If you build your own image
without that proxy, the healthcheck fails before the agent runs. See
<a href="/scaleapi/DrugDiscoveryBench/blob/main/docs/egress-hardening.md">docs/egress-hardening.md</a>.</p>
</blockquote>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Appendix: Grok CLI</h2><a id="user-content-appendix-grok-cli" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Appendix: Grok CLI" href="#appendix-grok-cli"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The official <a href="https://x.ai/cli" rel="nofollow">xAI Grok CLI</a> (npm <code>@xai-official/grok</code>, binary
<code>grok</code>) is baked into the image as of <code>2.10.3</code>. <strong>It is not yet a Harbor agent</strong> —
the installed Harbor has no <code>grok</code> adapter, so <code>--agent grok</code> does not work. Today
grok is only a runnable binary inside the container (manual / non-Harbor use):</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="docker run --rm -it -e GROK_API_KEY=your-key \
    ghcr.io/scaleapi/drugdiscoverybench:1.0.2-lightweight grok --help"><pre>docker run --rm -it -e GROK_API_KEY=your-key \
    ghcr.io/scaleapi/drugdiscoverybench:1.0.2-lightweight grok --help</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">It reads <code>GROK_API_KEY</code> and <code>GROK_BASE_URL</code> (default <code>https://api.x.ai/v1</code>; an
OpenAI-compatible proxy works). To drive Grok through the eval harness, a Harbor
<code>grok</code> adapter must land upstream first.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">License</h2><a id="user-content-license" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: License" href="#license"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>

the <a href="/scaleapi/DrugDiscoveryBench/blob/main/LICENSE">MIT License</a>. Scoring rubrics and reference answers are <strong>not</strong> in this
repo — they are distributed via the gated HuggingFace dataset
<a href="https://huggingface.co/datasets/ScaleAI/DrugDiscoveryBench" rel="nofollow"><code>ScaleAI/DrugDiscoveryBench</code></a>
(see Quick Start). The Docker image bundles third-party software and data — notably
<a href="https://github.com/snap-stanford/Biomni">BiOMNI</a> (Apache-2.0) and the baked
biomedical datasets — under their own licenses (see the image's <code>NOTICE</code>).</p>
